4   Commerzbank Interim Report as at 30 September 2020




       Performance highlights
       1 January to 30 September 2020
                             Key statements

                             • Commerzbank recorded stable customer business in the first nine months of 2020. In the Private and Small-
                                 Business Customers segment, the Bank benefited from a high level of trading activity and further expanded its
                                 customer base. Following the full takeover of comdirect, Commerzbank will realise the desired synergies by
                                 combining the services of one of Germany’s most modern online banks with personal advisory services at a
                                 local level. In the Corporate Clients segment, the International Corporates division benefited from very positive
                                 capital market business and a significant contribution from primary market business with bonds, which were
                                 offset by lower income from cash management and trade finance due to the pandemic. Overall, Commerzbank
                                 posted an operating profit of €94m in the first nine months of 2020 in a difficult business environment.


                             • The Group risk result was reported at €–1,067m. The significant increase by €–697m year on year was due in
                                 particular to the coronavirus pandemic and the impairment of a single loan commitment in the second quarter
                                 of 2020. The non-performing exposure (NPE) ratio was 0.9%.


                             • Operating expenses were reduced by 3.3% to €4,551m thanks to systematic cost management. Compulsory
                                 contributions, which include the European banking levy, contributions to the Deposit Protection Fund and the
                                 Polish bank tax, were reported separately and rose by around 15% to €445m, due primarily to changed
                                 calculation parameters.


                             • Provisions of €201m for restructuring expenses were recognised in the third quarter of 2020. Around 70% of
                                 this figure relates to the Bank’s renewed voluntary age-related short-time working scheme, while the
                                 remainder is connected to the previously announced streamlining of our branch network.


                             • The consolidated loss attributable to Commerzbank shareholders and investors in additional equity
                                 components was €–162m, compared with a profit of €681m in the prior-year period.


                             • The Common Equity Tier 1 ratio was 13.5%; the leverage ratio was 4.8% (fully loaded).

                             • The operating return on equity was 0.5%, compared with 5.6% in the prior-year period. The return on equity
                                 based on consolidated profit or loss (less intangible assets and AT1-related items) was –1.2%, compared with
                                 3.5% in the previous year. The cost/income ratio was 73.9% with compulsory contributions excluded and
                                 81.1% including compulsory contributions.



                             Performance of the Commerzbank share

                             In the first nine months of 2020, events on the international stock markets were overshadowed by the outbreak of
                             the coronavirus pandemic and the measures taken to contain it. In the wake of this unprecedented situation,
                             previously dominant geopolitical events receded into the background, including tensions in the trade conflict
                             between the USA and China, economic sanctions against Iran and unrest in the Middle East. Ongoing uncertainty
                             surrounding the further spread of the pandemic and its consequences for the real economy dampened medium-
                             term interest rate and inflation expectations both in the eurozone and worldwide. While the stock markets have
To our Shareholders                          Interim Management Report                     Interim Risk Report           Interim Financial Statements   5
4    Performance highlights




largely recovered over the course of the year following their dramatic slump in the first quarter of 2020, thanks
not least to emergency government aid for companies affected by the coronavirus pandemic and favourable long-
term refinancing options made available to banks by the European Central Bank (targeted longer-term refinancing
operations), European banks recorded significantly higher provisioning requirements for credit risks in the first
nine months of the year. Dividend payments in the sector were also suspended as a precautionary measure at the
recommendation of the European banking supervisory authorities. The DAX lost just 3.7% in the first nine months
of the year, while the EuroStoxx 50 fell by almost 15%. European banks, whose medium-term profit expectations
were hit by the effects of rising risk costs and economic uncertainties, recorded a much weaker performance that
was not offset on the income side despite the cheaper refinancing conditions. As the EuroStoxx Banks Index fell
by 43.7% in the first nine months of the year, the Commerzbank share price dropped by 24.0%. Stock market
indices recorded significant losses due to the rapidly evolving nature of the coronavirus pandemic and the
measures announced at the end of October to stem the rate of infection.

Important staffing and business policy events after the end
of the previous reporting period

Changes in the Board of Managing Directors of Commerzbank
Michael Mandel, who was previously the member of the Board of Managing Directors responsible for the Private
and Small-Business Customers segment, made an offer to the Supervisory Board to terminate his contract by
mutual agreement. At its meeting on 17 September 2020, the Supervisory Board of Commerzbank approved the
departure of Michael Mandel by mutual agreement. Michael Mandel’s term of office as a member of the Board of
Managing Directors therefore ended on 30 September 2020. Sabine Schmittroth assumed interim responsibility
for Private and Small-Business Customers at Group management level as of 1 October 2020, in addition to her
role as Director of Human Resources. Dr. Marcus Chromik, Chief Risk Officer, assumed responsibility for
Compliance as of the same date.
6   Commerzbank Interim Report as at 30 September 2020




                             New Chairman of the Board of Managing Directors of Commerzbank
                             At its meeting on 26 September 2020, the Supervisory Board of Commerzbank decided to appoint Dr. Manfred
                             Knof as Chairman of the Board of Managing Directors of Commerzbank. He will take up his position as of 1 January
                             2021, subject to the approval of the supervisory authorities. Dr. Manfred Knof was Chairman of the Board of
                             Deutsche Bank Privat- und Firmenkundenbank AG from 1 August 2019 until its merger with Deutsche Bank Group
                             and is currently Head of Deutsche Bank’s Private Bank Germany.


                             Commerzbank issues another Additional Tier 1 bond
                             At the beginning of September 2020, Commerzbank Aktiengesellschaft successfully issued the second Additional
                             Tier 1 bond under its issuance programme for Additional Tier 1 capital (AT1) under which it can issue successive
                             subordinated debt securities with a total nominal value of up to €3bn over the next few years. The bond has a
                             volume of €500m and a fixed coupon of 6.5% per annum. At over €2.4bn, the order book was heavily
                             oversubscribed, reflecting the widespread interest among investors. With the issue of the AT1 bond,
                             Commerzbank is further strengthening and optimising its capital structure. The AT1 bond has a perpetual maturity
                             and the first call date is in the period from October 2029 to April 2030. The bond terms provide for a temporary
                             write-down in the event that the Bank’s Common Equity Tier 1 ratio (CET1 ratio) drops below 5.125%.
                             Shareholders’ subscription rights were excluded. The successful issue of the second Additional Tier 1 bond
                             enabled the Bank to reduce its regulatory Common Equity Tier 1 requirement to 9.8%. As a result, the buffer for
                             the maximum distributable amount (MDA) was around 370 basis points as at the end of September of this year.
                             In the medium term, the Bank aims to maintain a distance of at least 200 basis points.


                             Commerzbank makes climate risks in its portfolio transparent and comparable
                             Since the beginning of September 2020, Commerzbank has been an official supporter of the Task Force on
                             Climate-related Financial Disclosures (TCFD). The TCFD recommendations form the basis for consistent and
                             comparable reporting on the impact of climate change. They provide a framework to help companies manage the
                             impacts of climate-related risks and produce the relevant disclosures. Commerzbank plans to publish its first
                             TCFD report in 2022, covering the 2021 financial year.


                             Commerzbank issues a second green bond
                             In mid-September 2020, Commerzbank very successfully issued another green bond with an issuance volume of
                             €500m. This is the Bank’s second green bond and follows the inaugural issue in October 2018. Commerzbank
                             will use the proceeds to finance renewable energy projects. The bond has a term of 5.5 years with a call date in
                             March 2025 and a coupon of 0.75% per annum. Commerzbank has earmarked the bond proceeds for loans for
                             onshore and offshore wind and solar projects in Germany, other European countries and North America. The
                             projects financed by the green bond will help cut CO2 emissions by around 850,000 tonnes per year. Commerzbank
                             has been a signatory of the Green Bond Principles since 2014. The Bank is a partner in the Climate Bonds Initiative
                             since January 2020.


                             Commerzbank completes full takeover of comdirect
                             Commerzbank has successfully completed the full takeover of comdirect bank Aktiengesellschaft (comdirect). The
                             merger was entered in the Commercial Register on 2 November 2020, formally ending comdirect’s independent
                             status and stock market listing. Following the merger, Commerzbank and comdirect will initially retain their
                             respective service offerings unchanged. The aim is to merge and expand the offerings of both banks. The new
                             joint business model has yet to be negotiated with the employee representative committees. Quickborn and
                             Rostock will remain as locations. The employment relationships of all comdirect employees as at the time of the
                             merger will be transferred to Commerzbank.

16   Commerzbank Interim Report as at 30 September 2020




        Liquidity in the secondary markets, which in this context is
     already significantly reduced, will thus remain restrained. Despite
     rising government debt, we expect German government bond yields
     to remain very low, even in the long-term segment (yields are
     currently negative up to nearly 30 years), and we anticipate
     persistently high demand from investors for high-quality securities.
     In view of this, we believe credit spreads will remain tight.



     Anticipated performance of the Commerzbank
     Group

     We essentially stand by the guidance we gave in the interim report
     as at 30 June 2020 regarding the Commerzbank Group’s anticipated
     earnings performance in 2020. However, the rapidly evolving
     nature of the coronavirus pandemic means that the form and impact
     of the response measures planned or decided by the federal and
     state governments will need to be monitored very closely over the
     coming days and weeks. This may in turn lead to corresponding
     reassessments of individual earnings forecasts.
        On balance, we expect to report a consolidated loss for the 2020
     financial year due to the ongoing difficult market environment and
     risk factors, the higher risk result and the provisions recognised for
     restructuring expenses.
        Based on the Common Equity Tier 1 ratio reported as at the
     reporting date and the expected change in risk-weighted assets and
     Tier 1 capital between now and the end of the year, we are
     anticipating a Common Equity Tier 1 ratio of at least 13% as at the
     end of 2020.

Notes to the income statement

(7) Net interest income


All interest income and interest expenses – including interest-like             As with interest income, interest expenses contain all interest ex-
income and expenses – are reported in this item, provided they do            penses, including reversals of premiums/discounts and other

     Other notes on financial instruments


     (28) IFRS 13 fair value hierarchies and disclosure requirements


     Fair value hierarchy                                                         All fair values are subject to the Commerzbank Group’s internal
     Under IFRS 13, financial instruments are assigned to the three lev-       controls and procedures, which set out the standards for inde-
     els of the fair value hierarchy as follows:                               pendently verifying or validating fair values. These controls and pro-
     • Level 1: Financial instruments where the fair value is based on         cedures are carried out and coordinated by the Independent Price
        quoted prices for identical financial instruments in an active         Verification (IPV) Group within the risk function. The models, inputs
        market.                                                                and resulting fair values are reviewed regularly by senior manage-
     • Level 2: Financial instruments where no quoted prices are avail-        ment and the risk function.
        able for identical instruments in an active market and the fair
        value is established using valuation techniques which rely on ob-      Disclosure obligations
        servable market parameters.                                            Below, a distinction is made between:
     • Level 3: Financial instruments where valuation techniques are           a) financial instruments measured at fair value (fair value OCI, fair
        used that incorporate at least one material input for which there is   value option, mandatorily fair value P&L and held for trading);
        insufficient observable market data and where at least this input      b) financial instruments measured at amortised cost.
        has a more than insignificant impact on the fair value.                   The respective disclosure requirements regarding these finan-
                                                                               cial instruments are set out in IFRS 7 and IFRS 13. For example,
        With respect to the methods of model-based measurements                they require explanatory statements on the valuation techniques ap-
     (level 2 and level 3) relevant for banks, IFRS 13 recognises the mar-     plied and the inputs used for levels 2 and 3, as well as quantitative

     or comparable assets and liabilities.                                     reclassifications between fair value hierarchy levels, reconciliations

      ity ratio. It is a measure of the near-term solvency of the Bank under    timely manner and mitigated through suitable measures. This also

      ulation EU/2015/61 (D-REG).                                                  Commerzbank manages its global liquidity centrally using cash

upon our current plans, estimates, forecasts and expectations. The statements entail risks and uncertainties, as there are a variety of
factors which influence our business and to a great extent lie beyond our sphere of influence. Above all, these include the economic
